not the case.\n        #\n        # When constructing a decoder sequence (`from_decoder_prompt` True)\n        # it does not matter whether `inputs` has its encoder-related\n        # member variables populated.\n        if not (from_decoder_prompt or is_encoder_decoder_inputs(inputs)):\n            raise ValueError(\"Cannot extract encoder input prompt from \"\n                             f\"invalid input {inputs}; did you forget the \"\n                             \"encoder input prompt fields?\")\n\n        self.data = SequenceData.from_seqs(self.prompt_token_ids)\n        self.output_logprobs: SampleLogprobs = []\n        self.output_text = \"\"\n\n        self.status = SequenceStatus.WAITING\n        self.stop_reason: Union[int, str, None] = None\n\n        # Discard the hidden states that are not used for sampling (i.e., all\n   155→        tokens except the final one in each prompt).\n   156→    2. Compute the logits for the next tokens.\n   157→    3. Apply presence, frequency and repetition penalties.\n   158→    4. Apply temperature scaling.\n   159→    5. Apply top-p and top-k truncation.\n   160→    6. Sample the next tokens.\n   161→    Here, each sequence group within the batch can have different sampling\n   162→    parameters (e.g., sampling method, temperature, top-p, top-k, etc.).\n   163→\n   164→    The structure of the logits tensor is coupled with the seq_groups in\n   165→    sampling_metadata. In this case, the sampling tensors logic depends on\n   237→            # \"output_tokens\" of a sequence. As a result, we cannot\n   238→            # reuse sampling tensors, since \"output_tokens\" changes\n   239→            # between decode runs.\n   240→            self._init_sampling_tensors(logits, sampling_metadata)\n   241→\n   242→        assert self._sampling_tensors is not None\n   243→        sampling_tensors = self._sampling_tensors\n   244→        do_penalties = self._do_penalties\n   245→        do_top_p_top_k = self._do_top_p_top_k\n   246→        do_min_p = self._do_min_p\n   247→\n   248→        logits = _apply_min_tokens_penalty(logits, sampling_metadata)\n   249→\n   250→        # Apply presence and frequency penalties.\n   251→        if do_penalties:\n   252→            logits = _apply_penalties(logits, sampling_tensors.prompt_tokens,\n   253→                                      sampling_tensors.output_tokens,\n   254→                                      sampling_tensors.presence_penalties,\n   255→                                      sampling_tensors.frequency_penalties,\n   256→                                      sampling_tensors.repetition_penalties)\n   257→\n   258→        # Use float32 to apply temperature scaling.\n   259→        # Use in-place division to avoid creating a new tensor.\n   260→        logits = logits.to(torch.float)\n   261→        logits.div_(sampling_tensors.temperatures.unsqueeze(dim=1))\n   262→\n   263→        if do_top_p_top_k and flashinfer_top_k_top_p_sampling is None:\n   264→            logits = _apply_top_k_top_p(logits, sampling_tensors.top_ps,\n   265→                                        sampling_tensors.top_ks)\n   266→\n   267→        if do_min_p:\n   268→            logits = _apply_min_p(logits, sampling_tensors.min_ps)\n   269→\n   270→        # We use float32 for probabilities and log probabilities.\n   271→        # Compute the probabilities.\n   272→        probs = torch.softmax(logits, dim=-1, dtype=torch.float)\n   273→        # Compute the log probabilities.\n   274→        logprobs = torch.log_softmax(logits, dim=-1, dtype=torch.float)\n   275→\n   276→        # Sample the next tokens.\n   277→        maybe_deferred_sample_results, maybe_sampled_tokens_tensor = _sample(\n   278→            probs,\n   279→            logprobs,\n   280→            sampling_metadata,\n   281→            sampling_tensors,\n   282→            include_gpu_probs_tensor=self.include_gpu_probs_tensor,\n   283→            modify_greedy_probs=self._should_modify_greedy_probs_inplace,\n   284→        )\n   285→\n   286→        if self.include_gpu_probs_tensor:\n   287→            # Since we will defer sampler result Pythonization,\n   288→            # preserve GPU-side tensors in support of later\n   289→            # deferred pythonization of logprobs\n   290→            assert maybe_sampled_tokens_tensor is not None\n   291→            on_device_tensors = (probs, logprobs, maybe_sampled_tokens_tensor)\n   292→        else:\n   293→            # Since Pythonization has already happened, don't preserve\n   294→            # GPU-side tensors.\n   295→            on_device_tensors = None\n   296→\n   297→        # Get the logprobs query results.\n   298→        prompt_logprobs = None\n   299→        sample_logprobs = None\n   300→        if not sampling_metadata.skip_sampler_cpu_output:\n   301→            # Pythonize logprobs now (GPU -> CPU); do not defer.\n   302→            assert not isinstance(maybe_deferred_sample_results,\n   303→                                  SampleResultArgsType)\n   304→            prompt_logprobs, sample_logprobs = get_logprobs(\n   305→                logprobs, sampling_metadata, maybe_deferred_sample_results)\n   306→\n   307→        return _build_sampler_output(\n   308→            maybe_deferred_sample_results,\n   309→            sampling_metadata,\n   310→            prompt_logprobs,\n   311→            sample_logprobs,\n   312→            on_device_tensors=on_device_tensors,\n   313→            skip_sampler_cpu_output=sampling_metadata.skip_sampler_cpu_output)\n   314→\n   315→    @property\n   316→    def _should_modify_greedy_probs_inplace(self) -> bool:\n   317→        \"\"\"Whether or not the sampler should modify the probability distribution\n   318→        of greedily-sampled tokens such that multinomial sampling would sample\n   319→        the greedily-sampled token.\n   320→\n   321→        In other words, if True then we set the probability of the greedily-\n   322→        sampled token to 1.\n   323→\n   324→        This is used by speculative decoding, which requires that the sampling\n   325→        method be encoded into the probability distribution.\n   326→        \"\"\"\n   327→        return self.should_modify_greedy_probs_inplace\n   328→\n   329→\n   330→def _get_bin_counts_and_mask(\n   331→    tokens: torch.Tensor,\n   332→    vocab_size: int,\n   333→    num_seqs: int,\n   334→) -> Tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:\n   335→    # Compute the bin counts for the tokens.\n   336→    # vocab_size + 1 for padding.\n   337→    bin_counts = torch.zeros((num_seqs, vocab_size + 1),\n   338→                             dtype=torch.long,\n   339→                             device=tokens.device)\n   340→    bin_counts.scatter_add_(1, tokens, torch.ones_like(tokens))\n   341→    bin_counts = bin_counts[:, :vocab_size]\n   342→    mask = bin_counts > 0\n   343→\n   344→    return bin_counts, mask\n   345→\n   346→\n   347→def _apply_min_tokens_penalty(\n   348→    logits: torch.Tensor,\n   349→    sampling_metadata: SamplingMetadata,\n   350→) -> torch.Tensor:\n   351→    \"\"\"Apply min_tokens penalty which sets stop tokens to -inf if min_tokens\n   352→        have not been generated yet\n   353→    \"\"\"\n   354→    # list of indices in logits that will be set to -inf\n   355→    logits_to_penalize: List[Tuple[int, int]] = []\n   356→    logits_applied = 0\n   357→    for seq_group in sampling_metadata.seq_groups:\n   358→        seq_ids = seq_group.seq_ids\n   359→        sampling_params = seq_group.sampling_params\n   360→\n   361→        sample_indices = seq_group.sample_indices\n   362→        logits_applied += len(sample_indices) + len(\n   363→            seq_group.prompt_logprob_indices)\n   364→        if not seq_group.do_sample:\n   365→            continue\n   366→\n   367→        start_idx = sample_indices[0]\n   368→        min_tokens = sampling_params.min_tokens\n   369→        token_ids_to_penalize = sampling_params.all_stop_token_ids\n   370→        if min_tokens > 0 and token_ids_to_penalize:\n   371→            seqs_to_penalize: List[int] = []\n   372→            for j, seq_id in enumerate(seq_ids):\n   373→                seq_data = seq_group.seq_data[seq_id]\n   374→                if len(seq_data.output_token_ids_array) < min_tokens:\n   375→                    seqs_to_penalize.append(j)\n   376→\n   377→            if seqs_to_penalize:\n   378→                # convert to the index into logits\n   379→                seqs_to_penalize = [start_idx + j for j in seqs_to_penalize]\n   380→                # itertools.product pairs each seq index with every token id\n   381→                logits_to_penalize.extend(\n   382→                    itertools.product(seqs_to_penalize, token_ids_to_penalize))\n   383→\n   384→    if logits_to_penalize:\n   385→        # use zip and * to group indices along each dimension\n   386→        # eg. [ (1,2), (1,3), (5,6) ] -> ( (1,1,5), (2,3,6) )\n   387→        logits[tuple(zip(*logits_to_penalize))] = -float(\"inf\")\n   388→\n   389→    # verifies that no rows in logits were missed unexpectedly\n   390→    assert logits_applied == logits.shape[0]\n   391→    return logits\n   392→\n   393→\n   394→def _apply_penalties(logits: torch.Tensor, prompt_tokens_tensor: torch.Tensor,\n   395→                     output_tokens_tensor: torch.Tensor,\n   396→                     presence_penalties: torch.Tensor,\n   397→                     frequency_penalties: torch.Tensor,\n   398→                     repetition_penalties: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:\n   399→    num_seqs, vocab_size = logits.shape\n   400→    _, prompt_mask = _get_bin_counts_and_mask(prompt_tokens_tensor, vocab_size,\n   401→                                              num_seqs)\n   402→    output_bin_counts, output_mask = _get_bin_counts_and_mask(\n   403→        output_tokens_tensor, vocab_size, num_seqs)\n   404→\n   405→    repetition_penalties = repetition_penalties[:, None].repeat(1, vocab_size)\n   406→    repetition_penalties[~(prompt_mask | output_mask)] = 1.0\n   407→    logits = torch.where(logits > 0, logits / repetition_penalties,\n   408→                         logits * repetition_penalties)\n   409→\n   410→    # We follow the definition in OpenAI API.\n   411→    # Refer to https://platform.openai.com/docs/api-reference/parameter-details\n   412→    logits -= frequency_penalties.unsqueeze_(dim=1) * output_bin_counts\n   413→    logits -= presence_penalties.unsqueeze_(dim=1) * output_mask\n   414→    return logits\n   415→\n   416→\n   417→def _apply_top_k_top_p(\n   418→    logits: torch.Tensor,\n   419→    p: torch.Tensor,\n   420→    k: torch.Tensor,\n   421→) -> torch.Tensor:\n   422→    logits_sort, logits_idx = logits.sort(dim=-1, descending=False)\n   423→\n   424→    # Apply top-k.\n   425→    top_k_mask = logits_sort.size(1) - k.to(torch.long)\n   426→    # Get all the top_k values.\n   427→    top_k_mask = logits_sort.gather(1, top_k_mask.unsqueeze(dim=1))\n   428→    top_k_mask = logits_sort < top_k_mask\n   429→    logits_sort.masked_fill_(top_k_mask, -float(\"inf\"))\n   430→\n   431→    # Apply top-p.\n   432→    probs_sort = logits_sort.softmax(dim=-1)\n   433→    probs_sum = probs_sort.cumsum(dim=-1)\n   434→    top_p_mask = probs_sum <= 1 - p.unsqueeze(dim=1)\n   435→    # at least one\n   436→    top_p_mask[:, -1] = False\n   437→    logits_sort.masked_fill_(top_p_mask, -float(\"inf\"))\n   438→\n   439→    # Re-sort the probabilities.\n   440→    logits = torch.empty_like(logits_sort).scatter_(dim=-1,\n   441→                                                    index=logits_idx,\n   442→                                                    src=logits_sort)\n   443→    return logits\n   444→\n   445→\n   446→def _apply_min_p(\n   447→    logits: torch.Tensor,\n   448→    min_p: torch.Tensor,\n   449→) -> torch.Tensor:\n   450→    \"\"\"\n   451→    Adapted from\n   452→    https://github.com/oobabooga/text-generation-webui/blob/3146124ec01f02c8fb1650a6517cf1b60b537aaf/modules/sampler_hijack.py#L16C17-L16C17\n   453→    \"\"\"\n   454→    probs = torch.softmax(logits, dim=-1)\n   455→    top_probs, _ = probs.max(dim=-1, keepdim=True)\n   456→    scaled_min_p = min_p.unsqueeze_(dim=1) * top_probs\n   457→    tokens_to_remove = probs < scaled_min_p\n   458→    logits = logits.masked_fill_(tokens_to_remove, -float(\"inf\"))\n   459→\n   460→    return logits\n   461→\n   462→\n   463→def _greedy_sample(\n   464→    selected_seq_groups: List[SequenceGroupToSample],\n   465→    samples: torch.Tensor,\n   466→) -> SampleResultType:\n   467→    \"\"\"Run greedy sampling on a given samples.\n   468→\n   469→    Args:\n   470→        selected_seq_groups: A list of sequence groups batched.\n   471→        samples: (num_selected_samples,) A tensor of samples. The length of\n   472→            samples could be smaller than selected_seq_groups if\n   473→            seq_group.do_sample is False.\n   474→    Returns:\n   475→        Tuple of (next_token_ids, parent_ids). The length of returned list is\n   476→        same as the length of selected_seq_groups. If the corresponding\n   477→        seq_group has do_sample=False, tuple contains ([], [])\n   478→    \"\"\"\n   479→    samples_lst = samples.tolist()\n   480→    sample_idx = 0\n   481→    results: SampleResultType = []\n   482→    for seq_group in selected_seq_groups:\n   483→        if not seq_group.do_sample:\n   484→            results.append(([], []))\n   485→            continue\n   486→\n   487→        seq_ids = seq_group.seq_ids\n   488→        num_parent_seqs = len(seq_ids)\n   489→        assert num_parent_seqs == 1, (\n   490→            \"Greedy sampling should have only one seq.\")\n   491→        parent_ids = list(range(num_parent_seqs))\n   492→        next_token_ids = [samples_lst[sample_idx]]\n   493→        results.append((next_token_ids, parent_ids))\n   494→        sample_idx += num_parent_seqs\n   495→    return results\n   496→\n   497→\n   498→def _random_sample(\n   499→    selected_seq_groups: List[SequenceGroupToSample],\n   500→    random_samples: torch.Tensor,\n   501→) -> SampleResultType:\n   502→    \"\"\"Run random sampling on a given samples.\n   503→\n   504→    Args:\n   505→        selected_seq_groups: A list of sequence groups batched.\n   506→        random_samples: (num_selected_samples,) A tensor of samples. The\n   507→            length of samples could be smaller than selected_seq_groups if\n   508→            seq_group.do_sample is False.\n   509→    Returns:\n   510→        Tuple of (next_token_ids, parent_ids). The length of returned list is\n   511→        same as the length of selected_seq_groups. If the corresponding\n   512→        seq_group has do_sample=False, tuple contains ([], [])\n   513→    \"\"\"\n   514→    # Find the maximum n value of the prompt phase requests.\n   515→    random_samples = random_samples.cpu()\n   516→    sample_idx = 0\n   517→    results: SampleResultType = []\n   518→    for seq_group in selected_seq_groups:\n   519→        if not seq_group.do_sample:\n   520→            results.append(([], []))\n   521→            continue\n   522→\n   523→        seq_ids = seq_group.seq_ids\n   524→        sampling_params = seq_group.sampling_params\n   525→        is_prompt = seq_group.is_prompt\n   526→        num_parent_seqs = len(seq_ids)\n   527→        if is_prompt:\n   528→            # Prompt phase.\n   529→            parent_ids = [0] * sampling_params.n\n   530→            next_token_ids = random_samples[\n   531→                sample_idx, :sampling_params.n].tolist()\n   532→        else:\n   533→            # Generation phase.\n   534→            parent_ids = list(range(num_parent_seqs))\n   535→            next_token_ids = random_samples[sample_idx:sample_idx +\n   536→                                            num_parent_seqs, 0].tolist()\n   537→        results.append((next_token_ids, parent_ids))\n   538→        sample_idx += num_parent_seqs\n   539→    return results\n   540→\n   541→\n   542→def _beam_search_sample(\n   543→    selected_seq_groups: List[SequenceGroupToSample],\n   544→    logprobs: torch.Tensor,\n   545→) -> SampleResultType:\n   546→    \"\"\"Run beam sampling on a given samples.\n   547→\n   548→    Args:\n   549→        selected_seq_groups: A list of sequence groups batched.\n   550→        logprobs: (num_selected_samples, vocab_size,) A tensor of logprob\n   551→        on selected sample indices.\n   552→    Returns:\n   553→        Tuple of (next_token_ids, parent_ids). The length of returned list is\n   554→        same as the length of selected_seq_groups. If the corresponding\n   555→        seq_group has do_sample=False, tuple contains ([], [])\n   556→    \"\"\"\n   557→    # We sample 2 * beam_width candidates to make sure that with high\n   558→    # probability we can get `beam_width` candidates in addition to\n   559→    # the finished sequences for the next iteration. See\n   560→    # https://github.com/tensorflow/tensor2tensor/blob/bafdc1b67730430d38d6ab802cbd51f9d053ba2e/tensor2tensor/utils/beam_search.py#L557-L563\n   561→    # for details. See also HF reference:\n   562→    # https://github.com/huggingface/transformers/blob/a4dd53d88e4852f023332d284ff07a01afcd5681/src/transformers/generation/utils.py#L3063-L3065\n   563→    #\n   564→    # NOTE: Beam search is not vectorized, so its speed can be slower than\n   565→    # other sampling methods.\n   566→    sample_idx = 0\n   567→    results: SampleResultType = []\n   568→    for seq_group in selected_seq_groups:\n   569→        if not seq_group.do_sample:\n   570→            results.append(([], []))\n   571→            continue\n   572→\n   573→        is_prompt = seq_group.is_prompt\n   574→        seq_ids, sampling_params = seq_group.seq_ids, seq_group.sampling_params\n   575→        num_parent_seqs = len(seq_ids)\n   576→        beam_width = sampling_params.n\n   577→        seq_group_logprobs = logprobs[sample_idx:sample_idx + num_parent_seqs]\n   578→        if is_prompt:\n   579→            # Prompt phase.\n   580→            assert num_parent_seqs == 1, (\n   581→                \"Prompt input should have only one seq.\")\n   582→            parent_ids = [0] * (2 * beam_width)\n   583→            _, next_token_ids = torch.topk(seq_group_logprobs[0],\n   584→                                           2 * beam_width)\n   585→            next_token_ids = next_token_ids.tolist()\n   586→        else:\n   587→            # Generation phase.\n   588→            cumulative_logprobs: List[float] = [\n   589→                seq_group.seq_data[seq_id].cumulative_logprob\n   590→                for seq_id in seq_ids\n   591→            ]\n   592→            cumulative_logprobs_tensor = torch.tensor(\n   593→                cumulative_logprobs,\n   594→                dtype=torch.float,\n   595→                device=seq_group_logprobs.device)\n   596→            seq_group_logprobs = (seq_group_logprobs +\n   597→                                  cumulative_logprobs_tensor.unsqueeze(dim=1))\n   598→            _, topk_ids = torch.topk(seq_group_logprobs.flatten(),\n   599→                                     2 * beam_width)\n   600→            topk_ids = topk_ids.tolist()\n   601→            vocab_size = seq_group_logprobs.size(-1)\n   602→            parent_ids = [i // vocab_size for i in topk_ids]\n   603→            next_token_ids = [i % vocab_size for i in topk_ids]\n   604→        results.append((next_token_ids, parent_ids))\n   605→        sample_idx += num_parent_seqs\n   606→    assert sample_idx == logprobs.size(0)\n   607→    return results\n   608→\n   609→\n   610→# torch.multinomial forces a GPU<->CPU sync.\n   611→# Therefore, we use an optimized implementation instead.\n   612→# Note that we always sample with replacement.\n   613→# probs will be modified in place, but this is fine, as we pass\n   614→# in a copy already.\n   615→def _multinomial(\n   616→    probs: torch.Tensor,\n   617→    num_samples: int,\n   618→    seq_groups: Optional[List[SequenceGroupToSample]] = None,\n   619→) -> torch.Tensor:\n   620→    if num_samples > 1:\n   621→        probs = probs.repeat_interleave(num_samples, dim=0)\n   622→    q = torch.empty_like(probs)\n   623→    if seq_groups is None:\n   624→        q.exponential_()\n   625→    else:\n   626→        sample_idx = 0\n   627→        for seq_group in seq_groups:\n   628→            seq_ids = seq_group.seq_ids\n   629→            stride = len(seq_ids) * num_samples\n   630→            assert seq_group.generator is not None\n   631→            q[sample_idx:sample_idx +\n   632→              stride].exponential_(generator=seq_group.generator)\n   633→            sample_idx += stride\n   634→    return probs.div_(q).argmax(dim=1).view(-1, num_samples)\n   635→\n   636→\n   637→def _top_k_top_p_multinomial_with_flashinfer(\n   638→        probs: torch.Tensor, top_ks: torch.Tensor, top_ps: torch.Tensor,\n   639→        num_samples: int, seq_groups: Optional[List[SequenceGroupToSample]]):\n   640→    max_top_k_round = 32\n   641→    if num_samples > 1:\n   642→        probs = probs.repeat_interleave(num_samples, dim=0)\n   643→        top_ks = top_ks.repeat_interleave(num_samples)\n   644→        top_ps = top_ps.repeat_interleave(num_samples)\n   645→    batch_size = probs.shape[0]\n   646→    uniform_samples = torch.empty((max_top_k_round, batch_size),\n   647→                                  device=probs.device)\n   648→    if seq_groups is None:\n   649→        uniform_samples.uniform_()\n   650→    else:\n   651→        sample_idx = 0\n   652→        for seq_group in seq_groups:\n   653→            seq_ids = seq_group.seq_ids\n   654→            stride = len(seq_ids) * num_samples\n   655→            assert seq_group.generator is not None\n   656→            uniform_samples[:, sample_idx:sample_idx +\n   657→                            stride].uniform_(generator=seq_group.generator)\n   658→            sample_idx += stride\n   659→    batch_next_token_ids, success = flashinfer_top_k_top_p_sampling(\n   660→        probs,\n   661→        uniform_samples,\n   662→        top_ks,\n   663→        top_ps,\n   664→    )\n   665→    if not success.all():\n   666→        warnings.warn(\"FlashInfer rejection sampling failed, fallback.\",\n   667→                      stacklevel=1)\n   668→        probs = flashinfer.sampling.top_k_renorm_prob(probs, top_ks)\n   669→        probs = flashinfer.sampling.top_p_renorm_prob(probs, top_ps)\n   670→        batch_next_token_ids = flashinfer.sampling.sampling_from_probs(\n   671→            probs, uniform_samples[0])\n   672→    return batch_next_token_ids.view(-1, num_samples)\n   673→\n   674→\n   675→def get_pythonized_sample_results(\n   676→        sample_result_args: SampleResultArgsType) -> SampleResultType:\n   677→    '''This function consumes GPU-side sampler results and computes\n   678→    Pythonized CPU-side sampler results (GPU -> CPU sync.)\n   679→\n   680→    Single-step scheduling: this function is invoked at sampling-time\n   681→    for immediate Pythonization.\n   682→\n   683→    Multi-step scheduling: Pythonization is deferred until after multiple\n   684→    GPU-side steps have been completed.\n   685→\n   686→    Args:\n   687→      sample_result_args: GPU-side inputs to the Pythonization process\n   688→\n   689→    Returns:\n   690→      Pythonized sampler results\n   691→    '''\n   692→\n   693→    (\n   694→        sample_metadata,\n   695→        sampling_metadata,\n   696→        greedy_samples,\n   697→        multinomial_samples,\n   698→        beam_search_logprobs,\n   699→        sample_results_dict,\n   700→    ) = (\n   701→        sample_result_args.sample_metadata,\n   702→        sample_result_args.sampling_metadata,\n   703→        sample_result_args.greedy_samples,\n   704→        sample_result_args.multinomial_samples,\n   705→        sample_result_args.beam_search_logprobs,\n   706→        sample_result_args.sample_results_dict,\n   707→    )\n   708→\n   709→    for sampling_type in SamplingType:\n   710→        if sampling_type not in sample_metadata:\n   711→            continue\n   712→        (seq_group_id, seq_groups) = sample_metadata[sampling_type]\n   713→        if sampling_type == SamplingType.GREEDY:\n   714→            sample_results = _greedy_sample(seq_groups, greedy_samples)\n   715→        elif sampling_type in (SamplingType.RANDOM, SamplingType.RANDOM_SEED):\n   716→            sample_results = _random_sample(seq_groups,\n   717→                                            multinomial_samples[sampling_type])\n   718→        elif sampling_type == SamplingType.BEAM:\n   719→            sample_results = _beam_search_sample(seq_groups,\n   720→                                                 beam_search_logprobs)\n   721→        sample_results_dict.update(zip(seq_group_id, sample_results))\n   722→\n   723→    return [\n   724→        sample_results_dict.get(i, ([], []))\n   725→        for i in range(len(sampling_metadata.seq_groups))\n   726→    ]\n   727→\n   728→\n   729→def _sample_with_torch(\n   730→    probs: torch.Tensor,\n   731→    logprobs: torch.Tensor,\n   732→    sampling_metadata: SamplingMetadata,\n   733→    sampling_tensors: SamplingTensors,\n   734→    include_gpu_probs_tensor: bool,\n   735→    modify_greedy_probs: bool,\n   736→) -> SampleReturnType:\n   737→    '''Torch-oriented _sample() implementation.\n   738→\n   739→    Single-step scheduling:\n   740→    * Perform GPU-side sampling computation\n   741→    * Immediately Pythonize sampling result\n   742→\n   743→    Multi-step scheduling:\n   744→    * Perform GPU-side sampling computation\n   745→    * Defer Pythonization & preserve GPU-side\n   746→      tensors required for Pythonization\n   747→    '''\n   748→\n   749→    categorized_seq_group_ids: Dict[SamplingType,\n   750→                                    List[int]] = {t: []\n   751→                                                  for t in SamplingType}\n   752→    categorized_sample_indices = sampling_metadata.categorized_sample_indices\n   753→    for i, seq_group in enumerate(sampling_metadata.seq_groups):\n   754→        sampling_params = seq_group.sampling_params\n   755→        sampling_type = sampling_params.sampling_type\n   756→                                      long_sample_indices,\n   800→                                             greedy_samples)\n   801→\n   802→        elif sampling_type in (SamplingType.RANDOM, sampling_params = seq_group.sampling_params\n   807→                    max_n_in_batch = max(max_n_in_batch, sampling_params.n)\n   808→            seq_groups_arg = (None if sampling_type == SamplingType.RANDOM else\n   809→                              seq_groups)\n   810→\n   811→            if flashinfer_top_k_top_p_sampling is not None:\n   812→                multinomial_samples[\n    seq_groups_arg,\n   819→                    )\n   820→            else:\n   821→                multinomial_samples[sampling_type] = _multinomial(\n   822→ 827→                # Store sampled tokens in output tensor.\n   828→                sampled_token_ids_tensor[long_sample_indices] = \\\n   829→                    multinomial_samples[sampling_type].to(torch.long)\n   830→\n   831→        elif sampling_type == SamplingType.BEAM:\n   832→            beam_search_logprobs = logprobs[sample_indices]\n   833→        else:\n   834→            raise ValueError(f\"Unsupported sampling type: {sampling_type}\")\n   835→\n   836→    # Encapsulate arguments for computing Pythonized sampler\n   837→    # results, whether deferred or otherwise.\n   838→    maybe_deferred_args = SampleResultArgsType(\n   839→        sampling_metadata=sampling_metadata,\n   840→        sample_metadata=sample_metadata,\n   841→        multinomial_samples=multinomial_samples,\n   842→        greedy_samples=greedy_samples,\n   843→        beam_search_logprobs=beam_search_logprobs,\n   844→        sample_results_dict=sample_results_dict)\n   845→\n   846→    if not sampling_metadata.skip_sampler_cpu_output:\n   847→        # GPU<->CPU sync happens here.\n   848→        # This also converts the        return (\n   856→            maybe_deferred_args,\n   857→            sampled_token_ids_tensor,\n   858→        )\n   859→\n   860→\n   861→def _sample(\n   862→    probs: torch.Tensor,\n _get_ranks(x: torch.Tensor, indices: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:\n   892→    \"\"\"\n   893→    This function calculates the ranks of the chosen tokens in a logprob tensor.\n   894→\n   895→    Args:\n   896→        x (torch.Tensor): 2D logprob tensor of shape (N, M)\n   897→                        where N is the no. of tokens and M is the vocab dim.\n   898→        indices (torch.Tensor): List of chosen token indices.\n   899→\n   900→    Returns:\n   901→        torch.Tensor: 1D tensor of shape (N,) where N is the no. of tokens.\n   902→                    Each element in the returned tensor represents the rank\n   903→                    of the chosen token in the input logprob tensor.\n   904→    \"\"\"\n   905→    vals = x[torch.arange(0, len(x), device=x.device, dtype=indices.dtype),\n   906→             indices]\n   907→    result = (x > vals[:, None])\n   908→    del vals\n   909→    return result.sum(1).add_(1)\n   910→\n   911→\n   912→def get_logprobs(\n   913→    logprobs: torch.Tensor,\n   914→    sampling_metadata: SamplingMetadata,\n   915→    sample_results: SampleResultType,\n   916→) -> Tuple[List[Optional[PromptLogprobs]], List[SampleLogprobs]]:\n   917→    \"\"\"Return sample logprobs and prompt logprobs.\n   918→\n   919→    The logic consists of 3 parts.\n   920→    - Select indices to compute logprob from, ranks of token ids, and\n   921→        the top k token ids from logprobs.\n   922→    - Compute prompt logprobs if required.\n   923→    - Compute sample logprobs if required.\n   924→\n   925→    Args:\n   926→        logprobs: (num_query_tokens_across_batch, num_vocab). Each query token's\n   927→            logprob per vocab. Sequence groups' query tokens are batched in a\n   928→            single flattened tensor. For example, assuming there are N\n   929→            seq groups, it is sorted by prefill tokens for seq_group_1 (if\n   930→            prompt logprob is enabled), decode tokens for seq_group_1 (if\n   931→            sampling is required), prefill tokens for seq_group_2, ...\n   932→        sampling_metadata: The sampling metadata.\n   933→        sample_results: (num_seq_groups) The tuple of (next_token_ids,\n   934→            parent_ids) for each sequence group. When beam search is enabled,\n   935→            sample_results can contain different number of seq_ids from\n   936→            sampling_metadata.seq_groups. It is because beam search creates\n   937→            2 * BEAM_WIDTH number of samples (whereas there are only up to\n   938→            BEAM_WIDTH number of seq_ids).\n   939→\n   940→    Returns:\n   941→        A tuple of prompt and sample logprobs per sequence group in a batch.\n   942→    \"\"\"\n   943→    # The index of query token to calculate logprobs. It includes both\n   944→    # prompt and sample logprob indices.\n   945→    query_indices: List[int] = []\n   946→    # The next token ids to get the logprob value from.\n   947→    next_token_ids: List[int] = []\n   948→    # The largest requested number of logprobs. We find logprobs as many as the\n   949→    # largest num logprobs in this API. If every logprobs is None, it will be\n   950→    # set to -1.\n   951→    largest_num_logprobs = -1\n   952→\n   953→    # Select indices to compute logprob from, ranks of token ids, and the top\n   954→    # k token ids from logprobs.\n   955→    for (seq_group, sample_result) in zip(sampling_metadata.seq_groups,\n   956→                                          sample_results):\n   957→        sampling_params = seq_group.sampling_params\n   958→\n   959→        # Update indices and tokens for prompt logprobs.\n   960→        if (seq_group.is_prompt\n   961→                and sampling_params.prompt_logprobs is not None):\n   962→            largest_num_logprobs = max(largest_num_logprobs,\n   963→                                       sampling_params.prompt_logprobs)\n   964→            next_prompt_tokens = _get_next_prompt_tokens(seq_group)\n   965→            query_indices.extend(seq_group.prompt_logprob_indices)\n   966→            next_token_ids.extend(next_prompt_tokens)\n   967→\n   968→        # Update indices and next tokenes for sample logprob.\n   969→        if seq_group.do_sample:\n   970→            token_ids, parent_seq_ids = sample_result\n   971→            # NOTE: We cannot directly use sample_indices because\n   972→            # sample_indices only contain parent seq_ids of a previous step.\n   973→            # The current step may have different number of seq_ids, and\n   974→            # we can obtain it from `sample_result[1]`.\n   975→            query_idx = seq_group.sample_indices[0]\n   976→            query_indices.extend(\n   977→                [query_idx + parent_id for parent_id in parent_seq_ids])\n   978→            next_token_ids.extend(token_ids)\n   979→\n   980→            if sampling_params.logprobs is not None:\n   981→                largest_num_logprobs = max(largest_num_logprobs,\n   982→                                           sampling_params.logprobs)\n   983→\n   984→        assert len(next_token_ids) == len(query_indices)\n   985→\n   986→    if len(query_indices) == 0:\n   987→        empty_sampled_logprob: SampleLogprobs = []\n   988→        empty_prompt_logprob: Optional[PromptLogprobs] = None\n   989→        return [empty_prompt_logprob], [empty_sampled_logprob]\n   990→\n   991→    selected_logprobs, ranks = None, None\n   992→    top_logprobs, top_token_ids = None, None\n   993→\n   994→    # If largest_num_logprobs == -1, i.e. no logprobs are requested, we can\n   995→    # skip the whole logprob calculation.\n   996→    if largest_num_logprobs >= 0:\n   997→        query_indices_gpu = torch.tensor(query_indices, device=logprobs.device)\n   998→        next_token_ids_gpu = torch.tensor(next_token_ids,\n   999→                                          device=logprobs.device)\n  1000→\n  1001→        # (num_selected_query_tokens, num_logprobs). Note that query_indices can\n  1002→        # contain duplicates if beam search is enabled.\n  1003→        selected_logprobs = logprobs[[\n  1004→            query_indices_gpu,\n  1005→            next_token_ids_gpu,\n  1006→        ]]\n  1007→        ranks = _get_ranks(\n  1008→            logprobs[query_indices_gpu],\n  1009→            next_token_ids_gpu,\n  1010→        )\n  1011→        assert selected_logprobs.shape[0] == ranks.shape[0]\n  1012→\n  1013→        # We need to compute top k only if there exists logprobs > 0.\n  1014→        if largest_num_logprobs > 0:\n  1015→            # Logprobs of topk tokens for a batch of sequence groups.\n  1016→            # (num_query_tokens_across_batch).\n  1017→            top_logprobs, top_token_ids = torch.topk(logprobs,\n  1018→                                                     largest_num_logprobs,\n  1019→                                                     dim=-1)\n  1020→            top_logprobs = top_logprobs.to('cpu')\n  1021→            top_token_ids = top_token_ids.to('cpu')\n  1022→\n  1023→        selected_logprobs = selected_logprobs.to('cpu')\n  1024→        ranks = ranks.to('cpu')\n  1025→\n  1026→    # Find prompt/sample logprobs.\n  1027→    prompt_logprobs_per_seq_group: List[Optional[PromptLogprobs]] = []\n  1028→    sample_logprobs_per_seq_group: List[SampleLogprobs] = []\n  1029→    top_logprob_idx = 0\n  1030→    selected_logprobs_idx = 0\n  1031→\n  1032→    for seq_group, sample_result in zip(sampling_metadata.seq_groups,\n  1033→                                        sample_results):\n  1034→        (prompt_logprobs, top_logprob_idx,\n  1035→         selected_logprobs_idx) = _get_prompt_logprob_if_needed(\n  1036→             seq_group, selected_logprobs, ranks, top_token_ids, top_logprobs,\n  1037→             selected_logprobs_idx, top_logprob_idx)\n  1038→        prompt_logprobs_per_seq_group.append(prompt_logprobs)\n  1039→\n  1040→        (sampled_logprobs, top_logprob_idx,\n  1041→         selected_logprobs_idx) = _get_sampled_logprob_if_needed(\n  1042→             seq_group, sample_result, selected_logprobs, ranks, top_token_ids,\n  1043→             top_logprobs, selected_logprobs_idx, top_logprob_idx)\n  1044→        sample_logprobs_per_seq_group.append(sampled_logprobs)\n  1045→\n  1046→    return prompt_logprobs_per_seq_group, sample_logprobs_per_seq_group\n  1047→\n  1048→\n  1049→def _get_prompt_logprob_if_needed(\n  1050→    seq_group: SequenceGroupToSample,\n  1051→    selected_logprobs: torch.Tensor,\n  1052→    ranks: torch.Tensor,\n  1053→    top_token_ids: torch.Tensor,\n  1054→    top_logprobs: torch.Tensor,\n  1055→    selected_logprobs_idx: int,\n  1056→    top_logprob_idx: int,\n  1057→):\n  1058→    \"\"\"Compute the prompt logprob from a sequence group if needed.\"\"\"\n  1059→    sampling_params = seq_group.sampling_params\n  1060→    is_prompt = seq_group.is_prompt\n  1061→\n  1062→    # Find prompt logprobs\n  1063→    prompt_logprobs: Optional[PromptLogprobs] = None\n  1064→    if is_prompt and sampling_params.prompt_logprobs is not None:\n  1065→        prompt_logprobs = []\n  1066→        num_logprobs = sampling_params.prompt_logprobs\n  1067→        next_prompt_tokens = _get_next_prompt_tokens(seq_group)\n  1068→        # Pre-select indexes and create a list. It is faster than calling .item\n  1069→        # repetitively.\n  1070→        selected_logprob_items = selected_logprobs[\n  1071→            selected_logprobs_idx:selected_logprobs_idx +\n  1072→            len(next_prompt_tokens)].tolist()\n  1073→        rank_items = ranks[selected_logprobs_idx:selected_logprobs_idx +\n  1074→                           len(next_prompt_tokens)].tolist()\n  1075→\n  1076→        for idx, token_id in enumerate(next_prompt_tokens):\n  1077→            # Calculate the prompt logprob of the real prompt tokens.\n  1078→            # {token_id: (logprob, rank_from_vocab)}\n  1079→            prompt_logprobs_dict: Dict[int, Tuple[float, int]] = {\n  1080→                token_id: (selected_logprob_items[idx], rank_items[idx])\n  1081→            }\n  1082→\n  1083→            # Add top K prompt logprobs along with its rank.\n  1084→            if num_logprobs > 0:\n  1085→                top_ids = top_token_ids[\n  1086→                    top_logprob_idx, :num_logprobs].tolist()\n  1087→                top_probs = top_logprobs[\n  1088→                    top_logprob_idx, :num_logprobs].tolist()\n  1089→                # Top K is already sorted by rank, so we can use 1 ~\n  1090→                # num_logprobs + 1 for rank.\n  1091→                top_ranks = range(1, num_logprobs + 1)\n  1092→                prompt_logprobs_dict.update({\n  1093→                    top_id: (top_prob, rank)\n  1094→                    for top_id, top_prob, rank in zip(top_ids, top_probs,\n  1095→                                                      top_ranks)\n  1096→                })\n  1097→            prompt_logprobs.append({\n  1098→                token_id: Logprob(*logprob_and_rank)\n  1099→                for token_id, logprob_and_rank in prompt_logprobs_dict.items()\n  1100→            })\n  1101→            # + 1 to go to the next prompt token.\n  1102→            top_logprob_idx += 1\n  1103→\n  1104→        # + len(next_prompt_tokens) to go to the next prompt.\n  1105→        selected_logprobs_idx += len(next_prompt_tokens)\n  1106→    return prompt_logprobs, top_logprob_idx, selected_logprobs_idx\n  1107→\n  1108→\n  1109→def _get_sampled_logprob_if_needed(\n  1110→    seq_group: SequenceGroupToSample,\n  1111→    sample_result: Tuple[List[int], List[int]],\n  1112→    selected_logprobs: torch.Tensor,\n  1113→    ranks: torch.Tensor,\n  1114→    top_token_ids: torch.Tensor,\n  1115→    top_logprobs: torch.Tensor,\n  1116→    selected_logprobs_idx: int,\n  1117→    top_logprob_idx: int,\n  1118→):\n  1119→    \"\"\"Compute the sample logprob if needed.\"\"\"\n  1120→    seq_ids = seq_group.seq_ids\n  1121→    num_logprobs = seq_group.sampling_params.logprobs\n  1122→    sampled_logprobs: SampleLogprobs = []\n  1123→    next_token_ids, parent_seq_ids = sample_result\n  1124→\n  1125→    if seq_group.do_sample:\n  1126→        assert len(next_token_ids) > 0\n  1127→        if num_logprobs is None:\n  1128→            for next_token_id in next_token_ids:\n  1129→                # Use a dummy logprob\n  1130→                sampled_logprobs.append({next_token_id: Logprob(inf)})\n  1131→        else:\n  1132→            # Pre-select items from tensor. tolist() is faster than repetitive\n  1133→            # `.item()` calls.\n  1134→            selected_logprob_items = selected_logprobs[\n  1135→                selected_logprobs_idx:selected_logprobs_idx +\n  1136→                len(next_token_ids)].tolist()\n  1137→            rank_items = ranks[selected_logprobs_idx:selected_logprobs_idx +\n  1138→                               len(next_token_ids)].tolist()\n  1139→            for idx, (next_token_id, parent_id) in enumerate(\n  1140→                    zip(next_token_ids, parent_seq_ids)):\n  1141→                # Get the logprob of a sampled token.\n  1142→                sampled_logprobs_dict = {\n  1143→                    next_token_id:\n  1144→                    (selected_logprob_items[idx], rank_items[idx])\n  1145→                }\n  1146→                if num_logprobs is not None and num_logprobs > 0:\n  1147→                    # Get top K logprobs.\n  1148→                    top_ids = top_token_ids[top_logprob_idx +\n  1149→                                            parent_id, :num_logprobs].tolist()\n  1150→                    top_probs = top_logprobs[\n  1151→                        top_logprob_idx + parent_id, :num_logprobs].tolist()\n  1152→                    # Top K is already sorted by rank, so we can use 1 ~\n  1153→                    # num_logprobs + 1 for rank.\n  1154→                    top_ranks = range(1, num_logprobs + 1)\n  1155→                    sampled_logprobs_dict.update({\n  1156→                        top_id: (top_prob, rank)\n  1157→                        for top_id, top_prob, rank in zip(\n  1158→                            top_ids, top_probs, top_ranks)\n  1159→                    })\n  1160→\n  1161→                sampled_logprobs.append({\n  1162→                    token_id: Logprob(*logprob_and_rank)\n  1163→                    for token_id, logprob_and_rank in\n  1164→                    sampled_logprobs_dict.items()\n  1165→                })\n  1166→\n  1167→        # NOTE: This part of code is not intuitive. `selected_logprobs` include\n  1168→        # logprobs for the current step, which has len(next_token_ids) tokens\n  1169→        # per sequence group. `logprobs` includes logprobs from the previous\n  1170→        # steps, which has len(seq_ids) tokens per sequence group.\n  1171→\n  1172→        # Iterate to the next sequence group in a batch.\n  1173→        selected_logprobs_idx += len(next_token_ids)\n  1174→        # Iterate to the next sequence group in a batch.\n  1175→        top_logprob_idx += len(seq_ids)\n  1176→    return sampled_logprobs, top_logprob_idx, selected_logprobs_idx\n  1177→\n  1178→\n  1179→def _modify_greedy_probs_inplace(logprobs: torch.Tensor, probs: torch.Tensor,\n  1180→                                 sample_indices: torch.Tensor,\n  1181→                                 greedy_samples: torch.Tensor) -> None:\n  1182→    \"\"\"Modify the probability distributions of the greedily-sampled tokens such\n  1183→    that each sampled token has a \"probability\" of 1.0. This is required by\n  1184→    speculative decoding, which depends on the sampling method being encoded\n  1185→    within the probability distribution for correctness.\n  1186→\n  1187→    # Why do we only need to do this for greedy sampling?\n  1188→\n  1189→    vLLM's sampler performs the following steps for greedy or multinomial\n  1190→    (random) sampling:\n  1191→        1. Get logits from model.\n  1192→        2. Modify logits according to per-sequence sampling parameters.\n  1193→            - Multiply by temperature, top-k and top-p masking, penalize tokens\n  1194→                according to their frequency, etc.\n  1195→        3. Sample a token.\n  1196→            - Random sampling simply samples from the modified probability\n  1197→                distribution.\n  1198→            - Greedy sampling performs `argmax` to obtain the token with the\n  1199→                highest likelihood.\n  1200→\n  1201→    Ignoring greedy sampling for a moment, we find that the computed probability\n  1202→    distribution has the following property: we can sample from it independently\n  1203→    and find that the token sampled by the Sampler has a frequency corresponding\n  1204→    to how often we see it in our sampling. In other words, for tokens sampled\n  1205→    with vLLM's random SamplingType, the computed probability distribution\n  1206→    encodes the sampling methodology completely.\n  1207→\n  1208→    Greedy sampling does not normally have this property. vLLM modifies logits\n  1209→    according to sampling params, then performs `argmax`, then returns the\n  1210→    sampled token and the computed probability distribution. If we sample from\n  1211→    the distribution, we'll find the likelihood of the greedily-sampled token\n  1212→    is not always 1.0.\n  1213→\n  1214→    Since lossless speculative decoding requires that the sampling methodology\n  1215→    be encoded within the probability distribution, we are motivated to modify\n  1216→    the probability distribution such that the sampled token has probability 1\n  1217→    when speculative decoding is used.\n  1218→\n  1219→    NOTE: Alternatively, we could use an extremely low temperature to achieve\n  1220→    greedy sampling using multinomial computation and unite the codepaths. This is required by\n    speculative decoding, which depends on the sampling method being encoded\n    within the probability distribution for correctness.\n\n    # Why do we only need to do this for greedy sampling?\n\n    vLLM's sampler performs the following steps for greedy or multinomial\n    (random) sampling:\n        1. Get logits from model.\n        2. Modify logits according to per-sequence sampling parameters.\n            - Multiply by temperature, top-k and top-p masking, penalize tokens\n                according to their frequency, etc.\n        3. Sample a token.\n            - Random sampling simply samples from the modified probability\n                distribution.\n            - Greedy sampling performs `argmax` to obtain the token with the\n                highest likelihood.\n\n    Ignoring greedy sampling for a moment, we find that the computed probability\n    distribution has the following property: we can sample from it independently\n    and find that the token sampled by the Sampler has a frequency corresponding\n    to how often we see it in our sampling. In other words, for tokens sampled\n    with vLLM's random SamplingType, the computed probability distribution\n    encodes the sampling methodology completely.\n\n    Greedy sampling does not normally have this property. vLLM modifies logits\n    according to sampling params, then performs `argmax`, then returns the\n    sampled token and the computed probability distribution. If we sample from\n    the distribution, we'll find the likelihood of the greedily-sampled token\n    is not always 1.0.\n\n    Since lossless speculative decoding requires that the sampling methodology\n    be encoded within the probability distribution, we are motivated to modify\n    the probability distribution such that the sampled token has probability 1\n    when speculative decoding is used.\n\n    NOTE: Alternatively, we could use an extremely low temperature to achieve\n    greedy sampling using multinomial computation and unite the codepaths. This\n    has implications on the overall design of the sampler, e.g. how to record\n    accurate logprobs for the user, so this improvement is deferred to later.\n    \"\"\"\n    # NOTE: logprobs are not modified so they can be returned to the user.\n    probs[sample_indices, :] = 0\n    probs[sample_indices, greedy_samples] = 1.0\n\n\ndef _build_sampler_output(\n    maybe_deferred_sample_results: MaybeDeferredSampleResultType,\n    sampling_metadata: SamplingMetadata,\n    prompt_logprobs: Optional[List[Optional[PromptLogprobs]]],\n    sample_logprobs: Optional[List[SampleLogprobs]],\n    on_device_tensors: Optional[Tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor,\n                                      torch.Tensor]],\n    skip_sampler_cpu_output: bool = False,\n) -> SamplerOutput:\n    \"\"\"Construct Python objects with the output of sampling.\n\n    Args:\n        on_device_tensors: Tuple containing on-device tensors with the\n            probabilities used in sampling and the sampled token ids. NOTE: Any python object stored here is not updated when it is\n    cuda-graph replayed. If you have values that need to be changed\n    dynamically, it should be stored in tensor. The tensor has to be\n    updated from `CUDAGraphRunner.forward` API.\n    \"\"\"\n    # (batch_size,). The sequence length per sequence. (Seq id -> list of physical block)\n    # E.g., [0, 1, 2] means tokens are stored in 0th, 1st, and 2nd blocks\n    # in the kv cache.    )\n        return self._cached_decode_metadata\n\n    def advance_step(self,\n                     model_input: \"ModelInputForGPUWithSamplingMetadata\",\n                     sampled_token_ids: Optional[torch.Tensor],\n                     block_size: int,\n                     num_seqs: int,\n                     num_queries: int,\n                     turn_prefills_into_decodes: bool = False):\n        \"\"\"\n        Update metadata in-place to advance one decode step.\n        \"\"\"\n        # When using cudagraph, the num_seqs is padded to the next captured\n        # batch sized, but num_queries tracks the actual number of requests in\n        # the batch. For --enforce-eager mode, num_seqs == num_queries\n        if num_seqs != num_queries:\n            assert num_seqs > num_queries\n            assert self.use_cuda_graph\n\n        if turn_prefills_into_decodes:\n            # When Mutli-Step is enabled with Chunked-Prefill, prefills and\n            # decodes are scheduled together. In the first step, all the\n            # prefills turn into decodes. This update reflects that\n            # conversion.\n            assert self.num_decode_tokens + self.num_prefills == num_seqs\n            self.num_decode_tokens += self.num_prefills\n            self.num_prefills = 0\n            self.num_prefill_tokens = 0\n            self.max_prefill_seq_len = 0\n            self.max_query_len = 1\n\n            self.slot_mapping = self.slot_mapping[:num_seqs]\n        else:\n            assert self.seq_lens is not None\n            assert self.max_decode_seq_len == max(self.seq_lens)\n\n        assert self.num_prefills == 0\n        assert self.num_prefill_tokens == 0\n        assert self.num_decode_tokens == num_seqs\n        assert self.slot_mapping.shape == (num_seqs, )\n\n        assert self.seq_lens is not None\n        assert len(self.seq_lens) == num_seqs\n        assert self.seq_lens_tensor is not None\n        assert self.seq_lens_tensor.shape == (num_seqs, )\n        assert self.max_query_len == 1\n        assert self.max_prefill_seq_len == 0\n\n        assert self.query_start_loc is not None\n        assert self.query_start_loc.shape == (num_queries + 1, )\n        assert self.seq_start_loc is not None\n        assert self.seq_start_loc.shape == (num_seqs + 1, )\n\n        assert self.context_lens_tensor is not None\n        assert self.context_lens_tensor.shape == (num_queries, )\n\n        assert self.block_tables is not None\n        assert self.block_tables.shape[0] == num_seqs\n\n        # Update query lengths. Note that we update only queries and not seqs,\n        # since tensors may be padded due to captured cuda graph batch size\n        for i in range(num_queries):\n            self.seq_lens[i] += 1\n        self.max_decode_seq_len = max(self.seq_lens)\n\n        ops.advance_step_flashattn(num_seqs=num_seqs,\n                                   num_queries=num_queries,\n                                   block_size=block_size,\n                                   input_tokens=model_input.input_tokens,\n                                   sampled_token_ids=sampled_token_ids,\n                                   input_positions=model_input.input_positions,\n                                   seq_lens=self.seq_lens_tensor,\n                                   slot_mapping=self.slot_mapping,\n                                   block_tables=self.block_tables)\n\n\nclass FlashAttentionMetadataBuilder(\n        AttentionMetadataBuilder[FlashAttentionMetadata]):\n\n    def __init__(self, input_builder: \"ModelInputForGPUBuilder\"):\n        self.slot_mapping: List[int] = []\n        self.prefill_seq_lens: List[int] = []\n        self.context_lens: List[int] = []\n        self.block_tables: List[List[int]] = []\n        self.curr_seq_lens: List[int] = []\n        self.num_prefills = 0\n        self.num_prefill_tokens = 0\n        self.num_decode_tokens = 0\n        self.has_prefix_cache_hit = False\n\n        self.input_builder = input_builder\n        self.runner = input_builder.runner\n        self.sliding_window = input_builder.sliding_window\n        self.block_size = input_builder.block_size\n        self.use_v2_block_manager = (\n            input_builder.scheduler_config.use_v2_block_manager)\n\n    def _add_seq_group(\n            self, inter_data: \"ModelInputForGPUBuilder.InterDataForSeqGroup\",\n            chunked_prefill_enabled: bool, prefix_cache_hit: bool):\n        \"\"\"Add a sequence group to the metadata. Specifically update/append\n        1. context length.\n        2. block table.\n        3. slot mapping.\n        \"\"\"\n        is_prompt = inter_data.is_prompt\n        block_tables = inter_data.block_tables\n\n        for (seq_id, token_len, seq_len, curr_seq_len, query_len, context_len,\n             curr_sliding_window_block) in zip(\n                 inter_data.seq_ids, [len(t) for t in inter_data.input_tokens],\n                 inter_data.orig_seq_lens, inter_data.seq_lens,\n                 inter_data.query_lens, inter_data.context_lens,\n                 inter_data.curr_sliding_window_blocks):\n            self.context_lens.append(context_len)\n\n            if is_prompt:\n                self.num_prefills += 1\n                self.num_prefill_tokens += token_len\n                self.prefill_seq_lens.append(seq_len)\n            else:\n                self.num_decode_tokens += query_len\n                self.curr_seq_lens.append(curr_seq_len)\n\n            # Compute block table.\n            # TODO(sang): Combine chunked prefill and prefix caching by\n            # only allowing multiple of block_size chunk size.\n            # NOTE: This only works for oooooooxxx style attention.\n            block_table = []\n            if prefix_cache_hit:\n                # NOTE(woosuk): For flash-attn, the block table should\n                # include the entries for the incoming prefill tokens.\n                block_table = block_tables[seq_id]\n            elif ((chunked_prefill_enabled or not is_prompt)\n                  and block_tables is not None):\n                if curr_sliding_window_block == 0:\n                    block_table = block_tables[seq_id]\n                else:\n                    block_table = block_tables[seq_id][\n                        -curr_sliding_window_block:]\n            self.block_tables.append(block_table)\n\n            # Compute slot mapping.\n            is_profile_run = is_block_tables_empty(block_tables)\n            start_idx = compute_slot_mapping_start_idx(\n                is_prompt, query_len, context_len, self.sliding_window,\n                self.use_v2_block_manager)\n            compute_slot_mapping(is_profile_run, self.slot_mapping, seq_id,\n                                 seq_len, context_len, start_idx,\n                                 self.block_size, inter_data.block_tables)\n\n    def _get_graph_runner_block_tables(\n            self, num_seqs: int,\n            block_tables: List[List[int]]) -> torch.Tensor:\n        # The shape of graph_block_tables is\n        # [max batch size, max context len // block size].\n        max_batch_size, max_blocks = self.runner.graph_block_tables.shape\n        assert max_batch_size >= num_seqs\n\n        graph_block_tables = self.runner.graph_block_tables[:num_seqs]\n        for i, block_table in enumerate(block_tables):\n            if block_table:\n                num_blocks = len(block_table)\n                if num_blocks <= max_blocks:\n                    graph_block_tables[i, :num_blocks] = block_table\n                else:\n                    # It may be possible to have more blocks allocated due\n                    # to lookahead slots of multi-step, however, they are\n                    # not used anyway, so can be safely ignored.\n                    graph_block_tables[\n                        i, :max_blocks] = block_table[:max_blocks]\n\n        return torch.from_numpy(graph_block_tables).to(\n            device=self.runner.device, non_blocking=True)\n\n    def build(self, seq_lens: List[int], query_lens: List[int],\n              cuda_graph_pad_size: int, batch_size: int):\n        \"\"\"Build attention metadata with on-device tensors.\n\n        Args:\n            seq_lens: The maybe padded sequence lengths of the input sequences.\n            query_lens: The query lengths of the input sequences.\n            cuda_graph_pad_size: The padding size for cuda graph.\n                                 -1 if cuda graph is not used.\n            batch_size: The maybe padded batch size.\n        \"\"\"\n        prefix_cache_hit = any([\n            inter_data.prefix_cache_hit\n            for inter_data in self.input_builder.inter_data_list\n        ])\n        for inter_data in self.input_builder.inter_data_list:\n            self._add_seq_group(inter_data,\n                                self.input_builder.chunked_prefill_enabled,\n                                prefix_cache_hit)\n\n        device = self.runner.device\n        use_captured_graph = cuda_graph_pad_size != -1\n\n        max_query_len = max(query_lens)\n        decode_query_lens = |<--------------- num_prefill_tokens ----------------->|\t\n    |<--prefill_0-->|<--prefill_1-->|...|<--prefill_N-1--->|\n\n    Otherwise, the layout is as follows:\t\n    |<----------------- num_decode_tokens ------------------>|\t\n    |<--decode_0-->|..........|<--decode_M-1-->|<--padding-->|\n\n    Generation tokens can contain padding when cuda-graph is used.\n    Currently, prompt ## Analysis Instructions

Please provide a comprehensive analysis of this agent run. Your analysis should be rigorous and suitable for inclusion in an academic paper.

### 1. Quantitative Assessment

Count and categorize the following from the agent output:

- **Tool Calls**: Count each type of tool/action used (bash commands, file edits, file reads, etc.)
- **Errors**: Count errors by category (syntax, runtime, API, timeout)
- **Iterations**: Count edit-test cycles (how many times the agent modified code and tested)
- **Exploration Steps**: Count how many steps were spent exploring vs. executing

### 2. Tool Usage Analysis

Analyze the logs to provide exact counts of tool usage. Group tools as follows:
- **bash**: Shell commands (ls, cd, python, etc.)
- **read**: File reading operations (Read, etc.)
- **editor**: File modification operations (Write, Edit, NotebookEdit, TodoWrite, etc.)
- **web_search**: Web search operations (WebSearch, BraveSearch, etc.)
- **search**: Codebase search operations (Grep, Glob, Find, etc.)
- **other**: Any other tools not matching above
- **other_details**: A dictionary mapping specific "other" tool names to their counts.
- **discussion**: Briefly explain if the tool usage seems matched to the counts, especially for "other".

### 3. Quality Scores (0-10 scale with detailed justification)

**IMPORTANT CALIBRATION GUIDELINES:**
- **0-2**: Complete failure - no meaningful attempt or catastrophic errors
- **3-4**: Poor - significant gaps, major mistakes, largely ineffective
- **5-6**: Average - partial success, some correct decisions but notable weaknesses
- **7-8**: Good - mostly correct approach with minor issues, solid execution
- **9**: Excellent - nearly optimal, only minor imperfections
- **10**: Exceptional - reserve ONLY for truly outstanding performance that exceeds expectations; should be rare (<5% of runs)

**Scoring discipline:**
- A score of 10/10 requires flawless execution AND going beyond requirements
- Most successful runs should score 6-8, not 9-10
- Be critical and specific about deficiencies even for good runs
- Consider what an ideal agent would do, not just whether the task was completed

For each score, provide:
- The numeric score (0-10, calibrated per above guidelines)
- A detailed justification explaining why you gave this score
- Specific evidence from the agent output

#### Code Understanding Score
Sub-components:
- Repository Navigation (0-10): How well did the agent explore and understand the codebase structure?
- Function Identification (0-10): Did the agent correctly identify the relevant functions to modify?
- Dependency Awareness (0-10): Did the agent understand how different parts of the code interact?

#### Task Alignment Score
Sub-components:
- Goal Comprehension (0-10): Did the agent correctly understand what optimization was needed?
- Constraint Adherence (0-10): Did the agent respect all given constraints?
- Output Relevance (0-10): Were the changes relevant to the performance goal?

#### Approach Quality Score
Sub-components:
- Strategy Coherence (0-10): Was there a clear, logical strategy?
- Exploration Efficiency (0-10): Was exploration focused or scattered?
- Decision Quality (0-10): Were key decisions well-reasoned?

#### Execution Quality Score
Sub-components:
- Edit Correctness (0-10): Were edits syntactically and semantically correct?
- Test Verification (0-10): Did the agent verify that changes worked?
- Error Recovery (0-10): How well did the agent handle failures?

### 3. Categorical Classification

Classify the agent's behavior:

#### Approach Category
Choose ONE:
- `systematic`: Methodical, step-by-step approach with clear plan
- `direct_edit`: Quick targeted changes with minimal exploration
- `exploration_heavy`: Extensive codebase exploration before acting
- `trial_error`: Iterative attempts without clear strategy
- `minimal`: Minimal viable changes, possibly incomplete

#### Tool Usage Pattern
Choose ONE:
- `bash_heavy`: Primarily used bash/shell commands
- `editor_focused`: Primarily used file editing tools
- `read_heavy`: Primarily read files without much editing
- `balanced`: Balanced mix of different tools

#### Failure Category (if the run failed)
Choose ONE or `none` if successful:
- `understanding`: Agent misunderstood the task or codebase
- `execution`: Agent understood but failed to execute correctly
- `timeout`: Agent ran out of time or steps
- `api_error`: Technical/API failures interrupted the agent
- `no_patch`: Agent completed but produced no meaningful changes
- `none`: The run was successful

### 4. Detailed Analysis

#### Key Decisions (list 3-5)
Identify the most important decisions the agent made and explain their impact.

#### Optimization Techniques Used
List any performance optimization techniques the agent attempted (e.g., algorithm changes, memory optimization, parallelization).

#### Missed Opportunities
What could the agent have done better? What obvious optimizations did it miss?

#### Error Recovery Strategy
How did the agent respond to errors? Was the recovery effective?

### 5. Recommendations

- What changes to the agent's prompting might improve performance?
- What additional capabilities would help this type of task?
- What patterns should be encouraged or discouraged?
